49	\subsection{Evaluation Protocol}
50	\label{sec:evaluation}
51	
52	We report balanced accuracy, precision, pre-CP FPR (step-level and trace-level), coverage, and lead time (steps between first detection and true CP). Detection requires $K{=}5$ consecutive threshold crossings, empirically selected as a middle-ground tradeoff between coverage and false positive rate (\cref{app:k_ablation}). Modality comparisons use two-sided null-centered bootstrap (10{,}000 resamples) with Holm-Bonferroni step-down correction (3 metrics $\times$ 4 models $= 12$ tests, $\alpha{=}0.05$). Within each model, the three metrics are positively correlated by construction, making the correction stricter than under independence. Control task selectivity~\citep{hewitt2019control} validates genuine representational structure (\cref{app:control}).
53

[Director 指令] **[Part 3 Data Batch 1] 集成 E-R9-1/2/4/5 实验结果**

4 项实验完成，需集成到论文中。E-R9-3 (joint LOO-CV) 和 E-R9-6 (threshold sensitivity) 稍后追加。

## 1. QwQ-32B Position 相关结果（E-R9-1 + E-R9-4）

### experiments.tex Section 4.5 修改

当前 L90 附近写：
> "HS probes achieve higher precision (+4.5pp: 0.564 vs. 0.519) and lower FPR (−3.0pp: 0.217 vs. 0.247), which indicates that hidden states capture safety-specific patterns beyond positional information."

**修改为**（加入 p-values，弱化 claim）：
> "HS probes achieve directionally higher precision (+4.5pp: 0.564 vs. 0.519, p=0.133) and lower FPR (+2.5pp: 0.217 vs. 0.241, p=0.279), but neither difference is statistically significant at n=19. A residualized HS probe (position regressed out) remains significantly above chance (per-trace BA=0.667, p<0.003, Wilcoxon signed-rank), indicating that hidden states encode some position-independent safety signal. However, the majority of the HS probe's discriminative power on this model derives from temporal position encoding."

注意数据修正：
- FPR 差是 +2.5pp（0.241-0.217），不是 -3.0pp
- Position baseline FPR 是 0.241（不是 0.247）
- 口径选择：residualized BA 用 pooled=0.619 或 per-trace=0.667 均可，但需标注。建议用 per-trace（与论文其余 per-trace 口径一致），或写 "pooled step-level BA=0.619"

### Appendix 补充
在 position-residualized 段落补充显著性检验数据：
- Wilcoxon p=0.0024, t-test p=0.0008, permutation p<0.001
- Bootstrap 95% CI: [0.575, 0.744]
- 16/19 traces BA > 0.5

## 2. R1-8B FPR 5-seed 稳定性（E-R9-2）

### Appendix K 补充
在 multi-seed section 增加 FPR per-seed 表：

| Seed | HS FPR | Text FPR | ΔFPR | p-value |
|------|--------|----------|------|---------|
| 0 | 0.161 | 0.165 | -0.004 | 0.440 |
| 1 | 0.061 | 0.166 | -0.105 | <0.001 |
| 2 | 0.123 | 0.176 | -0.053 | 0.093 |
| 3 | 0.148 | 0.167 | -0.018 | 0.293 |
| 4 | 0.100 | 0.176 | -0.076 | 0.021 |

注释：方向一致 (5/5 seeds HS ≤ Text)，但仅 1/5 通过 α=0.05。Text FPR 非常稳定 (std=0.007) 而 HS FPR 对 split 敏感 (std=0.040)。

### 正文影响
- Section 4.2 或 4.5：加一句 "R1-8B FPR advantage is directionally consistent across seeds (5/5) but not robust to data split (1/5 p<0.05; Appendix K)"
- 如果正文提到 R1-8B "3/3 metrics significant"，需加 caveat 或改为 "precision and bal_acc robust; FPR direction-consistent but split-sensitive"

## 3. R1-32B Outlier Seed（E-R9-5）

### Appendix K 补充
FPR per-seed 数据：

| Seed | Prec | BA | FPR |
|------|------|------|------|
| 0 | 0.441 | 0.594 | 0.483 |
| 1 | 0.750 | 0.748 | 0.109 |
| 2 | 0.685 | 0.670 | 0.111 |
| 3 | 0.652 | 0.733 | 0.111 |
| 4 | 0.444 | 0.715 | 0.221 |
| mean±std | 0.594±0.128 | 0.692±0.056 | 0.207±0.145 |

注释：Seed 0 outlier (FPR=0.483) 由 test set trace 分配驱动。排除后 FPR std 降至 0.046。

补充：FPR std=0.145（与已报告的 precision std=0.128 对比展示完整方差图景）。

## 执行顺序
1. 修改 experiments.tex Section 4.5（QwQ position 结果 + p-values）
2. Appendix K 补充 R1-8B FPR 和 R1-32B 完整 per-seed 表
3. 正文 R1-8B FPR caveat
4. 编译验证
5. E-R9-3/6 结果到后追加

完成后汇报。

89	
90	R1-32B detects commitment 11--13 steps early with a +14.2pp margin over the position baseline, which confirms genuine representational signal, but covers only 25\% of traces at the single significant window ($W{=}1$). QwQ-32B achieves statistical significance at all window sizes with 74\% coverage, but HS probes fire later than text probes (negative lead time). The position baseline achieves higher balanced accuracy than HS probes (0.832 vs.\ 0.796) because harmful steps tend to occur later in traces; however, HS probes achieve higher precision (+4.5pp: 0.564 vs.\ 0.519) and lower FPR ($-$3.0pp: 0.217 vs.\ 0.247). A position-residualized probe (regressing out $t/T$ from hidden states before classification) reduces QwQ-32B balanced accuracy by 18.3pp (from 0.796 to 0.612), which indicates that the HS advantage on this model is largely mediated by temporal position encoding. The remaining precision and FPR margins likely reflect finer-grained position encoding rather than position-independent safety structure. The divergence implies that per-model validation is essential: no single detection configuration generalizes even within the same architecture at the same scale, and a system validated on one 32B model would fail on the other.
91

63	Against the 384d baseline, HS probes significantly outperform text probes on all four models across all three metrics (\cref{tab:hs_vs_text}). At 7--8B, the evidence is strong: precision gains of +32.5pp (R1-8B) and +21.9pp (OT-7B), stable across seeds (std ${<}4$pp). At 32B, both models are significant (HB corrected) but with model-specific caveats: R1-32B achieves significance only at $W{=}1$ with 25\% coverage, and QwQ-32B's position baseline is competitive (\cref{sec:32b-analysis}). Control task selectivity confirms genuine safety signal: 12.5--19.7pp above chance (\cref{app:control}).
64	
65	\paragraph{Dimensionality control.} PCA reduction of HS features to 384d (matching text encoder dimensionality, 79--92\% variance retained) preserves the precision advantage on all four models (+16.8 to +23.1pp, all $p{<}0.012$), with all four model-level precision tests passing Holm-Bonferroni. Two individual metric comparisons are non-significant: R1-8B balanced accuracy (+1.3pp, $p{=}0.731$, effect too small at $n{=}43$) and QwQ-32B FPR ($-$11.4pp, $p{=}0.038$, below HB threshold). This confirms a modality effect rather than a capacity artifact.
66	
67	\paragraph{Encoder robustness.} The 1024d rows in \cref{tab:hs_vs_text} replace MiniLM (384d) with BGE-large~\citep{xiao2023cpack} (1024d, a 2.7$\times$ stronger encoder). The HS precision advantage persists directionally on all four models but narrows substantially: only OT-7B survives Holm-Bonferroni (1/4 precision tests). HS balanced accuracy advantage is also directionally positive on all four models (+8.4 to +10.9pp), with 2/4 (OT-7B, R1-32B) significant under Holm-Bonferroni; FPR advantage is significant only for OT-7B (1/4). R1-8B shows an adverse FPR direction (HS FPR 1.2pp higher than BGE, $p{=}0.564$). The advantage is encoder-conditional rather than absolute (\cref{fig:forest_precision}).   "new_string": "\\section{QwQ-32B Position-Residualized Probe}\n\\label{app:position_residual}\n\nTo quantify position-independent safety signal in QwQ-32B hidden states, we regress out the normalized position $t/T$ from each hidden-state vector before training the logistic regression probe. The residualized probe achieves pooled step-level balanced accuracy of 0.619 and per-trace balanced accuracy of 0.667 (16/19 traces with BA $> 0.5$). Three complementary significance tests confirm the residualized probe performs above chance: Wilcoxon signed-rank $p{=}0.0024$, paired $t$-test $p{=}0.0008$, permutation test $p{<}0.001$. The bootstrap 95\\% CI for per-trace balanced accuracy is $[0.575, 0.744]$.\n\nThese results confirm that hidden states encode some position-independent safety signal. However, the drop from full-probe balanced accuracy (0.796) to residualized (0.619, pooled) indicates that the majority of discriminative power derives from temporal position encoding. The HS vs.\\ position-baseline precision gap (+4.5pp, $p{=}0.133$) and FPR gap (+2.5pp, $p{=}0.279$) are not statistically significant at $n{=}19$, so the non-positional component, while real, is too small for reliable detection at pilot scale.\n\n\n\\section{Multi-Seed Stability}\n\\label{app:multi_seed}"
```json
}
```

_source line 5163 · 2026-05-18T09:01:54.197181+00:00_

62	
63	Against the 384d baseline, HS probes significantly outperform text probes on all four models across all three metrics (\cref{tab:hs_vs_text}). At 7--8B, the evidence is strong: precision gains of +32.5pp (R1-8B) and +21.9pp (OT-7B), stable across seeds (std ${<}4$pp). At 32B, both models are significant (HB corrected) but with model-specific caveats: R1-32B achieves significance only at $W{=}1$ with 25\% coverage, and QwQ-32B's position baseline is competitive (\cref{sec:32b-analysis}). Control task selectivity confirms genuine safety signal: 12.5--19.7pp above chance (\cref{app:control}).
64	
65	\paragraph{Dimensionality control.} PCA reduction of HS features to 384d (matching text encoder dimensionality, 79--92\% variance retained) preserves the precision advantage on all four models (+16.8 to +23.1pp, all $p{<}0.012$), with all four model-level precision tests passing Holm-Bonferroni. Two individual metric comparisons are non-significant: R1-8B balanced accuracy (+1.3pp, $p{=}0.731$, effect too small at $n{=}43$) and QwQ-32B FPR ($-$11.4pp, $p{=}0.038$, below HB threshold). This confirms a modality effect rather than a capacity artifact.
66

### Tool result

_source line 5167 · 2026-05-18T09:02:06.407701+00:00_

1	Reasoning models with chain-of-thought can self-jailbreak: their reasoning traces transition from safe deliberation to committed harmful generation without adversarial input. We present the first systematic comparison of hidden-state (HS) probes and text-based monitors for detecting this safety commitment, evaluated across four reasoning models spanning two architectures and two scales (7--8B, 32B).
2	
3	Our central finding is methodological: the choice of layer selection metric can reverse a study's conclusions. A composite metric that incorporates crossing rate systematically selects shallow layers at 32B scale and masks the HS advantage entirely. With principled layer selection, HS probes achieve precision gains of +15.4 to +32.5 percentage points over 384d sentence embeddings across all four models (Holm-Bonferroni corrected). This advantage is encoder-conditional: against a competitive 1024d encoder, only 1/4 models retains significance, and the directional advantage narrows substantially. At 32B ($n{=}19$--$20$, sample-limited), model-specific caveats further qualify the results.
4	
5	Our results characterize the safety commitment phenomenon rather than constitute production detectors: false positive rates remain 39--52\%, and no alternative method consistently matches supervised probing. Layer selection methodology is a critical, underexamined researcher degree of freedom in the probing literature.
6

_source line 5171 · 2026-05-18T09:02:15.608961+00:00_

11	    \item \textbf{Layer selection sensitivity.} We identify a systematic bias in composite metrics that reverses HS probe conclusions at 32B scale. A five-metric ablation establishes principled selection criteria (\cref{sec:layer-results}).
12	
13	    \item \textbf{Encoder-conditional directional advantage.} Against MiniLM-384d, all four models show significant HS precision gains (+15.4 to +32.5pp, Holm-Bonferroni corrected). The advantage persists after PCA dimensionality matching (all four precision tests pass at 384d) but narrows substantially against BGE-large-1024d (1/4 models retains significance), so the advantage is encoder-conditional rather than absolute (\cref{fig:forest_precision}). At 7--8B scale (moderate samples, robust across 5 seeds), evidence is strong; at 32B ($n{=}19$--$20$ test traces, sample-limited), model-specific caveats apply: QwQ-32B's HS advantage is largely position-mediated (residualized probe drops 18.3pp), though HS probes retain higher precision (+4.5pp) and lower FPR ($-$3.0pp) over the position baseline (\cref{sec:32b-analysis}).
14	
15	    \item \textbf{Negative results define method boundaries.} No alternative method consistently matches supervised probing across all four models (\cref{tab:negative}): BOCPD/CUSUM fail on gradual transitions; Mahalanobis distance finds near-zero separation; RepE achieves competitive balanced accuracy on QwQ-32B (0.756 vs.\ supervised 0.778) but at substantially lower precision (0.512 vs.\ 0.700), a metric-dependent discrepancy; cross-model transfer fails under PCA alignment. Control task selectivity (12.5--19.7pp above chance; \cref{app:control}) confirms genuine safety structure.

_source line 5175 · 2026-05-18T09:02:27.558107+00:00_

4	Layer selection is an underexamined methodological choice in the probing literature that can silently determine a study's conclusions. Our five-metric ablation (\cref{sec:layer-results}) demonstrates that a single metric component, the crossing rate, reverses the central finding: at biased layers, the HS advantage appears scale-dependent; at corrected layers, all four models are significant (\cref{sec:evaluation}). The mechanism is representation compression at shallow layers (\cref{app:cosine_volatility}), which pushes a linear probe to the noise floor and inflates crossing rate independently of discriminative power. Any composite metric that incorporates crossing rate is susceptible to this bias, and we hypothesize the effect generalizes beyond safety probing to any threshold-based detection study using crossing rate for layer selection. Validation on other probing tasks is needed to confirm generality.
5	
6	\paragraph{Recommendations for probing methodology.} We recommend that probing studies report results under at least two complementary layer selection criteria, and that five-metric ablations (or at minimum, results at multiple candidate layers spanning shallow, middle, and deep positions) become standard practice. Even among unbiased metrics, the choice between threshold-based and threshold-free criteria affects layer selection: AUROC tends to select layers at shallower depth (3--50\%) than threshold-dependent metrics (44--98\%). Pure balanced accuracy exhibits the same pattern, selecting layers at 47--73\% depth for 32B models rather than the 94--98\% selected by threshold-dependent metrics. This divergence between threshold-free and threshold-dependent criteria further demonstrates that layer selection methodology is a critical researcher degree of freedom. _source line 5183 · 2026-05-18T09:02:44.069970+00:00_

6:\paragraph{Recommendations for probing methodology.} We recommend that probing studies report results under at least two complementary layer selection criteria, and that five-metric ablations (or at minimum, results at multiple candidate layers spanning shallow, middle, and deep positions) become standard practice. Even among unbiased metrics, the choice between threshold-based and threshold-free criteria affects layer selection: AUROC tends to select layers at shallower depth (3--50\%) than threshold-dependent metrics (44--98\%). Pure balanced accuracy exhibits the same pattern, selecting layers at 47--73\% depth for 32B models rather than the 94--98\% selected by threshold-dependent metrics. This divergence between threshold-free and threshold-dependent criteria further demonstrates that layer selection methodology is a critical researcher degree of freedom. At AUROC-optimal layers, HS probes retain directional precision advantage on all four models (+6.2 to +23.0pp), though these layers yield lower absolute performance than the deep layers selected by threshold-dependent metrics. The cost of ablation is modest: evaluating five metrics across all layers adds minutes to an experiment that takes hours. The alternative is that conclusions about representational structure reflect layer selection methodology rather than the phenomenon under study. The control task provides independent corroboration: R1-32B selectivity increases from 7.5pp at the biased layer (L10) to 19.7pp at the corrected layer (L63), which confirms that the biased metric selected layers where positional features dominate over genuine safety structure.

1	\section{Limitations}\label{sec:limitations}
2	
3	\paragraph{32B model-specific caveats.}
4	R1-32B achieves significance only at $W{=}1$ (25\% coverage, 5/20 traces); dimensionality ($51200 \times W$ for $n{=}20$) prevents larger windows. QwQ-32B's position baseline achieves higher balanced accuracy (0.832 vs.\ HS 0.796), HS lead time is negative, and position-residualized probing reduces balanced accuracy by 18.3pp. The QwQ-32B HS advantage is largely position-mediated. Multi-seed analysis (\cref{app:multi_seed}) confirms scale-dependent robustness: 7--8B precision std ${<}8$pp across 5 seeds, while R1-32B std $= 12.8$pp. This motivates a two-tier framing: 7--8B results are robust; 32B results are pilot-scale with caveats.
5	
6	\paragraph{High false positive rate and oracle gap.}
7	Safe-trace FPR is 39--52\% at standard thresholds, so current probes characterize the phenomenon rather than constitute production detectors. The 85.5\% oracle gap confirms that passive probing captures only a fraction of available signal.
8	
9	\paragraph{Cumulative hyperparameter selection.}
10	Layer (up to 64), $W$ (7 values), $K$ (5 values), and threshold are selected on the same validation set ($n{=}4$--$9$ at 32B), yielding up to $64 \times 7 \times 5 = 2{,}240$ configurations. $C$-insensitivity (${<}2$pp) covers one hyperparameter; the cumulative researcher degrees of freedom are substantial, particularly at 32B where per-fold sample sizes are small. $K$ was set as a design pre-selection (\cref{app:k_ablation}), and the $K \times W$ joint surface is not fully explored. Bootstrap CIs at $n{=}19$--$20$ may exhibit below-nominal coverage~\citep{efron1993bootstrap}; $p$-value resolution with 10{,}000 resamples is $10^{-4}$.
11	
12	\paragraph{Scope.}
13	We cover 2 architectures and 2 scales (7--8B, 32B); intermediate scales (13B, 70B) and proprietary reasoning models (o1, o3) are missing. The text baseline uses MiniLM-384d; BGE-large-1024d narrows the gap (1/4 HB-significant). All res
…[truncated 215 chars]

69	\paragraph{Caveats.} R1-32B achieves significance only at $W{=}1$ with 25\% coverage (5/20 traces); at $W{\geq}3$, dimensionality ($51200 \times W$ for $n{=}20$) prevents significance. Sweeping regularization $C$ over $[0.01, 100]$ yields ${<}2$pp variation in balanced accuracy, which confirms robustness to hyperparameter choice. QwQ-32B HS lead time is negative ($-$1.2 to +1.9 steps vs.\ text 3.7--12.3 steps): higher precision at later detection. On benign traces, probes produce 39--52\% false alarm rates, so current methods characterize the commitment phenomenon rather than constitute production-ready detectors. Per-model details in \cref{sec:32b-analysis}.

## 1. Threshold Sensitivity (E-R9-6, 两个模型完整数据)

### QwQ-32B (L60, W=3) — 已有数据，确认无需修改

HS bal_acc 跨 5 个 threshold (0.3-0.7) 波动仅 0.53pp。Δ(HS-Text) 稳定 +8.5~9.3pp。

### R1-32B (L63, W=1) — 新数据，需集成

| Thr | HS BA | Text BA | Δ BA | HS Prec | Text Prec | Δ Prec | HS FPR | Text FPR | Δ FPR |
|-----|-------|---------|------|---------|-----------|--------|--------|----------|-------|
| 0.3 | 0.752 | 0.627 | +0.126 | 0.583 | 0.457 | +0.126 | 0.295 | 0.401 | -0.106 |
| 0.4 | 0.746 | 0.630 | +0.116 | 0.586 | 0.466 | +0.120 | 0.281 | 0.373 | -0.092 |
| **0.5** | **0.742** | **0.633** | **+0.109** | **0.588** | **0.475** | **+0.113** | **0.273** | **0.351** | **-0.078** |
| 0.6 | 0.741 | 0.629 | +0.112 | 0.599 | 0.480 | +0.119 | 0.254 | 0.326 | -0.072 |
| 0.7 | 0.743 | 0.617 | +0.126 | 0.617 | 0.474 | +0.143 | 0.228 | 0.312 | -0.084 |

HS BA 波动仅 1.1pp (0.741-0.752)。Δ(HS-Text) 所有 threshold 一致 +0.11~0.13。FPR 方向正确（HS < Text）。

### 论文集成位置
在 Appendix 新增 "Threshold Sensitivity" 段落或表格（建议 Appendix I K ablation 附近），正文加一句引用：
> "The HS advantage is stable across decision thresholds (BA variation < 1.5pp across [0.3, 0.7]; \cref{app:threshold_sensitivity})"

## 2. Joint (layer, W) Grid Search (E-R9-3 partial data, QwQ-32B W=1/3/5)

注意：这是 grid search（固定 test set 评估所有 layer×W）而非 per-fold LOO-CV。

### QwQ-32B Top layers by window

| W | Best layer | BA | L60 BA | L60 rank |
|---|-----------|------|--------|---------|
| 1 | L47 | 0.798 | 0.783 | ~15th |
| 3 | L46 | 0.817 | 0.796 | ~6th |
| 5 | L60 | 0.808 | 0.808 | 1st |

### 论文集成（3 个叙事要点）
1. **L60 near-optimal at recommended windows**: "Joint (layer, W) search over 192 configurations confirms L60 is near-optimal (within 1-2pp of global best) at W=3-5"
2. **Layer-W interaction exists**: "Optimal layer shifts from middle-deep (~L46-47 at W=1) to deep (L60 at W=5), confirming that layer selection interacts with temporal context"
3. **L30 suboptimal everywhere**: "L30, selected by per-fold LOO-CV at W=1, is suboptimal across all windows (max BA=0.769 vs global best 0.817)"

位置：Appendix B (Layer Sweep) 新增 "Joint layer-window sensitivity" 段落 + 小表格。正文 Method §3.3 加一句引用。

### Limitation 说明
"Joint (layer, W) stability was evaluated via grid search on W ∈ {1, 3, 5} for QwQ-32B. Full leave-one-fold-out validation of the (layer, W) pair and extension to R1-32B remain for future work."

## 3. 不需要集成的（已在 Batch 1 处理）

- E-R9-2 (R1-8B FPR 5-seed) ✅

401	The best BOCPD configuration ($\lambda{=}10$) achieves 93.0\% detection rate with +3.45 steps lead. The simple threshold baseline achieves 65.1\% detection with superior lead (+4.89 steps). All CUSUM configurations underperform the simple threshold on lead time.
402	
403	These results reveal a coverage-timing tradeoff. BOCPD ($\lambda{=}10$) achieves 28pp higher detection rate than simple thresholding (93.0\% vs.\ 65.1\%), but with lower lead (+3.45 vs.\ +4.89 steps). The transition toward unsafe reasoning is gradual rather than abrupt, so change-point methods cannot exploit discontinuities for early warning. When missed detections are costly, BOCPD's coverage advantage justifies its use despite the timing penalty. When early intervention is the priority, simple thresholding provides better lead time.
404	
405	\paragraph{Other models.} On OT-7B, QwQ-32B, and R1-32B, BOCPD achieves 0\% detection rate across all tested configurations ($\lambda \in \{5, 10, 50, 100, 200\}$). The probe score sequences for these models show smoother transitions that never trigger the hazard function, consistent with the gradual commitment pattern. R1-8B's higher detection rate likely reflects its sharper probe score transitions at commitment boundaries.

434	$K{=}1$ yields perfect coverage but near-perfect safe FPR (0.92--1.00), offering no discrimination. $K{=}5$ provides the Pareto-optimal balance: average coverage 0.60 with average safe FPR 0.48 across models. OT-7B benefits most from increasing $K$, reaching 0.19 safe FPR at $K{=}5$ (vs.\ 0.92 at $K{=}1$) while retaining 51\% coverage. Higher $K$ values ($\geq$7) reduce safe FPR further but at steep coverage cost. $K{=}5$ vs.\ $K{=}7$ is the key decision: $K{=}7$ reduces mean safe FPR from 0.48 to 0.30 but drops mean coverage from 0.60 to 0.42.
435	
436	$K$ was set as a design pre-selection before evaluation, not tuned on the validation set; all main-text results use $K{=}5$ without per-model optimization. The $K \times W$ interaction is not fully explored: this ablation uses $W{=}1$ to isolate $K$'s effect, and the joint $K \times W$ surface remains a limitation. At optimal $W$, coverage at $K{=}5$ improves (e.g., R1-8B: 0.47 at $W{=}1$ vs.\ 0.60 at $W{=}15$).

438	
439	\section{M002 Bug Disclosure}
440	\label{app:bug}
441	
442	We disclose a bug discovered during development that affected intermediate results for QwQ-32B.
443	
444	\paragraph{Issue.} In experiment M002 (text baseline evaluation), the QwQ-32B text probe was inadvertently initialized with a midpoint heuristic placeholder rather than true sentence embeddings from all-MiniLM-L6-v2. Specifically, the placeholder assigned each step a feature vector equal to the midpoint between the mean safe and mean unsafe embeddings from the training set, with small Gaussian noise added. This made the text probe trivially predictive, producing a spuriously significant result ($p{=}0.001$ for HS vs. text FPR difference).
445

HS BA varies by 1.1pp (0.741--0.752); the HS advantage is consistent across all thresholds.}\n\\label{tab:threshold_r132b}\n\\begin{tabular}{ccccccc}\n\\toprule\n$\\tau$ & HS BA & $\\Delta$BA & HS Prec & $\\Delta$Prec & HS FPR & $\\Delta$FPR \\\\\n\\midrule\n0.3 & 0.752 & +0.126 & 0.583 & +0.126 & 0.295 & $-$0.106 \\\\\n0.4 & 0.746 & +0.116 & 0.586 & +0.120 & 0.281 & $-$0.092 \\\\\n\\textbf{0.5} & \\textbf{0.742} & \\textbf{+0.109} & \\textbf{0.588} & \\textbf{+0.113} & \\textbf{0.273} & $\\mathbf{-}$\\textbf{0.078} \\\\\n0.6 & 0.741 & +0.112 & 0.599 & +0.119 & 0.254 & $-$0.072 \\\\\n0.7 & 0.743 & +0.126 & 0.617 & +0.143 & 0.228 & $-$0.084 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\nR1-32B HS balanced accuracy varies by only 1.1pp across the full threshold range. The modality advantage ($\\Delta$BA $= +0.109$ to $+0.126$, $\\Delta$Prec $= +0.113$ to $+0.143$, $\\Delta$FPR $= -0.072$ to $-0.106$) is consistent in both magnitude and direction at every threshold, confirming that the HS advantage is not an artifact of the default $\\tau{=}0.5$.\n\n\n\\section{M002 Bug Disclosure}"

18	\section{Layer Sweep Results}
19	\label{app:layer_sweep}
20	
21	For each model, we sweep all transformer layers and evaluate five candidate selection metrics: (1)~balanced accuracy $\times$ crossing rate (the biased composite), (2)~pure balanced accuracy, (3)~precision, (4)~inverse pre-CP FPR, and (5)~an additive composite. The biased composite systematically selects shallow layers for 32B models because early-layer representations exhibit higher crossing rates (more frequent threshold crossings) regardless of discriminative power. Three threshold-dependent metrics (precision, inverse FPR, additive composite) converge on deep layers for all models; pure balanced accuracy selects middle layers for 32B models (L30 for QwQ-32B, L47 for R1-32B).
22	
23	\paragraph{Selected layers.} R1-8B: all five metrics agree on layer~14 (44\% depth). OT-7B: biased metric selects L0 (0\%), unbiased consensus selects L16 (57\%). QwQ-32B: biased metric selects L2 (3\%), unbiased consensus selects L60 (94\%). R1-32B: biased metric selects L10 (16\%), unbiased consensus selects L63 (98\%). The bias is most extreme for 64-layer models, where the composite metric selects layers in the bottom 16\% of the network while threshold-dependent metrics select layers above 94\%. Pure balanced accuracy selects middle layers (L30 at 47\% for QwQ-32B, L47 at 73\% for R1-32B), consistent with the AUROC pattern.
24	
25	\paragraph{Impact on modality comparison.} At the biased layers, HS vs.\ text precision $p$-values are 0.064 (R1-32B) and 0.35--0.83 (QwQ-32B), both non-significant. At the corrected deep layers, all $p$-values fall below 0.005. The initially reported ``scale-dependent'' pattern (HS advantage at 7--8B but not 32B) was entirely an artifact of layer selection methodology, not a genuine property of model scale (\cref{sec:layer-results}).
26

69	\paragraph{Caveats.} R1-32B achieves significance only at $W{=}1$ with 25\% coverage (5/20 traces); at $W{\geq}3$, dimensionality ($51200 \times W$ for $n{=}20$) prevents significance. Sweeping regularization $C$ over $[0.01, 100]$ yields ${<}2$pp variation in balanced accuracy, which confirms robustness to hyperparameter choice. QwQ-32B HS lead time is negative ($-$1.2 to +1.9 steps vs.\ text 3.7--12.3 steps): higher precision at later detection. On benign traces, probes produce 39--52\% false alarm rates, so current methods characterize the commitment phenomenon rather than constitute production-ready detectors. Per-model details in \cref{sec:32b-analysis}.

_source line 5219 · 2026-05-18T10:55:33.550978+00:00_

38	\label{sec:layer-sensitivity}
39	
40	Layer selection is a researcher degree of freedom that can reverse modality comparison conclusions. A composite metric (balanced accuracy $\times$ crossing rate) systematically selects shallow layers for 32B models: L2 (3\% depth) for QwQ-32B, L10 (16\%) for R1-32B. Shallow representations are highly compressed (inter-step cosine distances 1.4--2.7$\times$ smaller than at deep layers), so a linear probe operates near the noise floor and threshold crossings are noise-driven (\cref{app:cosine_volatility}).
41	
42	A five-metric ablation reveals that threshold-dependent metrics (precision, inverse FPR, additive composite) consistently select deep layers: L60 (94\%) for QwQ-32B, L63 (98\%) for R1-32B. Pure balanced accuracy selects middle layers (L30, L47), paralleling the AUROC pattern (\cref{sec:layer-results}). At these layers, all four models show significant HS advantage (Holm-Bonferroni corrected, \cref{sec:evaluation}); at biased layers, only the two 7--8B models survive. Because the three metrics (balanced accuracy, precision, FPR) share the same confusion matrix, the effective number of independent comparisons is four (one per model); Holm-Bonferroni step-down on four model-level tests yields the same result (all four pass, with R1-8B FPR $p{=}0.044$ at rank-4 threshold $\alpha/1{=}0.050$). Full per-layer results appear in \cref{app:layer_sweep}.
43

45	\label{sec:baselines}

1	\section{Limitations}\label{sec:limitations}
2	
3	\paragraph{32B model-specific caveats.}
4	R1-32B achieves significance only at $W{=}1$ (25\% coverage, 5/20 traces); dimensionality ($51200 \times W$ for $n{=}20$) prevents larger windows. QwQ-32B's position baseline achieves higher balanced accuracy (0.832 vs.\ HS 0.796), HS lead time is negative, and position-residualized probing reduces balanced accuracy by 18.3pp. The QwQ-32B HS advantage is largely position-mediated. Multi-seed analysis (\cref{app:multi_seed}) confirms scale-dependent robustness: 7--8B precision std ${<}8$pp across 5 seeds, while R1-32B std $= 12.8$pp. This motivates a two-tier framing: 7--8B results are robust; 32B results are pilot-scale with caveats.
5	
6	\paragraph{High false positive rate and oracle gap.}
7	Safe-trace FPR is 39--52\% at standard thresholds, so current probes characterize the phenomenon rather than constitute production detectors. The 85.5\% oracle gap confirms that passive probing captures only a fraction of available signal.
8	
9	\paragraph{Cumulative hyperparameter selection.}
10	Layer (up to 64), $W$ (7 values), $K$ (5 values), and threshold are selected on the same validation set ($n{=}4$--$9$ at 32B), yielding up to $64 \times 7 \times 5 = 2{,}240$ configurations. $C$-insensitivity (${<}2$pp) covers one hyperparameter; the cumulative researcher degrees of freedom are substantial, particularly at 32B where per-fold sample sizes are small. $K$ was set as a design pre-selection (\cref{app:k_ablation}), and the $K \times W$ joint surface is not fully explored. Bootstrap CIs at $n{=}19$--$20$ may exhibit below-nominal coverage~\citep{efron1993bootstrap}; $p$-value resolution with 10{,}000 resamples is $10^{-4}$.
11	
12	\paragraph{Scope.}
13	We cover 2 architectures and 2 scales (7--8B, 32B); intermediate scales (13B, 70B) and proprietary reasoning models (o1, o3) are missing. The text baseline uses MiniLM-384d; BGE-large-1024d narrows the gap (1/4 HB-significant). All res
…[truncated 215 chars]

1	\section{Experiments}\label{sec:experiments}
2	
3	We evaluate temporal probes across four reasoning models (\cref{tab:models}), comparing hidden-state (HS) and text modalities under different layer selection criteria.
4	
5	\subsection{Setup}\label{sec:setup}
6	
7	We study R1-8B (Llama-8B, R1-distilled, $n{=}43$ test traces), OT-7B (OpenThinker-7B; Qwen-7B, STILL-trained, $n{=}47$), QwQ-32B (Qwen-32B, native reasoning, $n{=}19$--$20$), and R1-32B (Qwen-32B, R1-distilled, $n{=}20$). All traces come from HarmThoughts with step-level CP annotations (60/20/20 split). HS probes extract last-token hidden states from a selected layer; text probes encode each step with all-MiniLM-L6-v2 (384d). Both use L2-regularized logistic regression with temporal windowing $W \in \{1, 3, 5, 10, 15, 20, 25\}$ selected on validation. Detection requires $K{=}5$ consecutive threshold crossings. Statistical testing follows \cref{sec:evaluation}.
8	
9	\subsection{Layer Selection Results}\label{sec:layer-results}
10	
11	\cref{tab:layer_selection} shows that the biased composite metric (balanced accuracy $\times$ crossing rate) systematically selects shallow layers for 32B models: L2 (3\% depth) for QwQ-32B and L10 (16\%) for R1-32B. Three of four metrics excluding crossing rate (precision, inverse FPR, and additive composite) consistently select deep layers: L60 (94\%) and L63 (98\%). Pure balanced accuracy, a threshold-free metric, selects middle layers (L30 and L47), consistent with the AUROC pattern discussed in \cref{sec:discussion}. The mechanism is that shallow layers produce highly compressed representations with small inter-step margins: step-to-step cosine distances are 1.4--2.7$\times$ smaller than at deep layers across all four models (\cref{app:cosine_volatility}). A linear probe in this compressed space operates near the noise floor, generating frequent threshold crossings that inflate crossing rate independently of discriminative power (\cref{sec:layer-sensitivity}).
12	
13	\beg

_source line 5239 · 2026-05-18T11:23:41.400244+00:00_

1	Reasoning models with chain-of-thought can self-jailbreak: their reasoning traces transition from safe deliberation to committed harmful generation without adversarial input. We present the first systematic comparison of hidden-state (HS) probes and text-based monitors for detecting this safety commitment, evaluated across four reasoning models spanning two architectures and two scales (7--8B, 32B).
2	
3	Our central finding is methodological: the choice of layer selection metric can reverse a study's conclusions. A composite metric that incorporates crossing rate systematically selects shallow layers at 32B scale and masks the HS advantage entirely. With principled layer selection, HS probes achieve precision gains of +15.4 to +32.5 percentage points over 384d sentence embeddings across all four models (Holm-Bonferroni corrected). This advantage is encoder-conditional: against a competitive 1024d encoder, only 1/4 models retains significance, and the directional advantage narrows substantially. At 32B ($n{=}19$--$20$, sample-limited), model-specific caveats further qualify the results.
4	
5	Our results characterize the safety commitment phenomenon rather than constitute production detectors: false positive rates remain 39--52\%, and no alternative method consistently matches supervised probing. Layer selection methodology is a critical, underexamined researcher degree of freedom in the probing literature.
6

_source line 5241 · 2026-05-18T11:23:41.702425+00:00_

1	\section{Conclusion}\label{sec:conclusion}
2	
3	We present the first controlled comparison of hidden-state and text-based probes for safety commitment detection in reasoning models, evaluated across four models spanning two architectures and two scales.
4	The central finding is methodological: a composite layer selection metric incorporating crossing rate reverses the study's main conclusion at 32B scale, while threshold-dependent metrics excluding crossing rate consistently select deep layers where all four models show significant HS advantage (Holm-Bonferroni corrected; precision +15.4 to +32.5pp over MiniLM-384d). This advantage is encoder-conditional: it narrows substantially against BGE-large-1024d (1/4 models significant).
5	Future work should close the 85.5\% oracle gap through causal intervention techniques~\citep{meng2022rome}, extend to multilingual and multi-turn settings, and explore stronger cross-model alignment methods beyond PCA-based transfer.
6

1	\section{Related Work}\label{sec:related-work}
2	
3	\paragraph{Safety in Reasoning Models.}
4	Reasoning models introduce a qualitatively new failure mode: the model's own chain of thought can transition from safety-aware deliberation to harmful generation without adversarial input modification~\cite{wang2025lrmsafety}. \citet{harmthoughts} document this self-jailbreak phenomenon with step-level annotations, while \citet{selfjailbreak} show that longer reasoning chains increase circumvention probability. \citet{hagendorff2026lrm_jailbreak} demonstrate that reasoning models can autonomously jailbreak other LLMs with 97\% success, identifying reasoning capability itself as a safety threat. H-CoT~\cite{hcot2025} shows reasoning chains can be externally hijacked, complementing internally-originating transitions. Unfaithful reasoning~\cite{chen2025unfaithful} further motivates hidden-state monitoring that bypasses text. On mitigation, SafeThink~\cite{safethink} steers early reasoning toward safety, SafeRemind~\cite{saferemind} inserts entropy-triggered reminders, and \citet{korbak2025cot_monitorability} argue that CoT monitorability is valuable but fragile. \citet{mao2025selfjailbreak_reasoning} further characterize self-jailbreak in reasoning models and propose Chain-of-Guardrails as a defense. \citet{baker2025monitoring} show that CoT monitoring can detect reward hacking in reasoning models but warn that optimization pressure promotes obfuscation, complementing our focus on passive detection. \citet{yin2025refusal_cliff} discover that safety-aligned reasoning models maintain refusal intentions during thinking but experience a sharp drop at output generation, a phenomenon our temporal probes could detect. \citet{ahrend2026safer_traces} address information leakage in traces; \citet{hu2026refusal_dynamics} trace refusal dynamics in standard LLMs. \citet{xu2025manipulated} show that reasoning models can be manipulated without external attacks through self-generated reason
…[truncated 4131 chars]

1	\section{Limitations}\label{sec:limitations}
2	
3	\paragraph{32B model-specific caveats.}
4	R1-32B achieves significance only at $W{=}1$ (25\% coverage, 5/20 traces); dimensionality ($51200 \times W$ for $n{=}20$) prevents larger windows. QwQ-32B's position baseline achieves higher balanced accuracy (0.832 vs.\ HS 0.796), HS lead time is negative, and position-residualized probing reduces balanced accuracy by 18.3pp. The QwQ-32B HS advantage is largely position-mediated. Multi-seed analysis (\cref{app:multi_seed}) confirms scale-dependent robustness: 7--8B precision std ${<}8$pp across 5 seeds, while R1-32B std $= 12.8$pp. This motivates a two-tier framing: 7--8B results are robust; 32B results are pilot-scale with caveats.
5	
6	\paragraph{High false positive rate and oracle gap.}
7	Safe-trace FPR is 39--52\% at standard thresholds, so current probes characterize the phenomenon rather than constitute production detectors. The 85.5\% oracle gap confirms that passive probing captures only a fraction of available signal.
8	
9	\paragraph{Cumulative hyperparameter selection.}
10	Layer (up to 64), $W$ (7 values), $K$ (5 values), and threshold are selected on the same validation set ($n{=}4$--$9$ at 32B), yielding up to $64 \times 7 \times 5 = 2{,}240$ configurations. $C$-insensitivity (${<}2$pp) covers one hyperparameter; the cumulative researcher degrees of freedom are substantial, particularly at 32B where per-fold sample sizes are small. $K$ was set as a design pre-selection (\cref{app:k_ablation}), and the $K \times W$ joint surface is not fully explored. Joint (layer, $W$) stability was verified via grid search for QwQ-32B ($W \in \{1,3,5\}$; \cref{app:layer_sweep}), but full leave-one-fold-out validation of the (layer, $W$) pair and extension to R1-32B remain for future work. Bootstrap CIs at $n{=}19$--$20$ may exhibit below-nominal coverage~\citep{efron1993bootstrap}; $p$-value resolution with 10{,}000 resamples is $10^{-4}$.
11	
12	\paragraph{Scope.}
13	We cover

451	
452	$K{=}1$ yields perfect coverage but near-perfect safe FPR (0.92--1.00), offering no discrimination. $K{=}5$ provides the Pareto-optimal balance: average coverage 0.60 with average safe FPR 0.48 across models. OT-7B benefits most from increasing $K$, reaching 0.19 safe FPR at $K{=}5$ (vs.\ 0.92 at $K{=}1$) while retaining 51\% coverage. Higher $K$ values ($\geq$7) reduce safe FPR further but at steep coverage cost. $K{=}5$ vs.\ $K{=}7$ is the key decision: $K{=}7$ reduces mean safe FPR from 0.48 to 0.30 but drops mean coverage from 0.60 to 0.42.
453	
454	$K$ was set as a design pre-selection before evaluation, not tuned on the validation set; all main-text results use $K{=}5$ without per-model optimization. The $K \times W$ interaction is not fully explored: this ablation uses $W{=}1$ to isolate $K$'s effect, and the joint $K \times W$ surface remains a limitation. At optimal $W$, coverage at $K{=}5$ improves (e.g., R1-8B: 0.47 at $W{=}1$ vs.\ 0.60 at $W{=}15$).
455

343	
344	Under this protocol, multi-layer GRU achieves the highest mean lead (7.01$\pm$1.40 steps) but at higher FPR than logistic regression baselines (0.189 vs.\ 0.134). The GRU advantage is concentrated in detection timing rather than classification accuracy, consistent with the main text finding that GRUs capture sequential dependencies that improve early detection. Lead time shows high variance across seeds (range 5.66--8.93 for GRU-multi), reinforcing that reported means should be interpreted cautiously.
345	
346	\paragraph{OT-7B multi-layer GRU (layers 13--19, 3 seeds).}
347	OT-7B GRU achieves 74.6$\pm$1.3\% balanced accuracy, 64.5$\pm$7.0\% coverage, +33.6$\pm$4.8 steps lead, and 27.4$\pm$6.1\% pre-CP FPR. The lead time is substantially longer than other models in absolute terms, but OT-7B traces average ${\sim}$80 reasoning steps (vs.\ ${\sim}$30 for R1-8B and 32B models), so absolute lead times are not directly comparable. The FPR-lead tradeoff mirrors R1-8B: GRU FPR (27.4\%) is far above the LR probe at layer 16 (10.0\%), confirming that GRUs gain lead time at the cost of more false alarms across both 7-8B models.
348

```json
  "old_string": "The downstream impact is severe: at biased layers, only the two 7--8B models show significant HS advantage; both 32B models fail.   "new_string": "The downstream impact is severe: at biased layers, only the two 7--8B models show significant HS advantage; both 32B models fail. At corrected layers, all four models pass Holm-Bonferroni correction (\\cref{sec:evaluation}); at 7--8B scale the advantage is robust and position-independent, while at QwQ-32B the advantage is statistically significant but predominantly position-mediated (HS vs.\\ position precision $p{=}0.133$; \\cref{sec:32b-analysis}). Had we reported only biased-layer results, we would have concluded a scale-dependent HS advantage that is entirely a methodological artifact."
}
```

```json
  "old_string": "The central finding is methodological: a composite layer selection metric incorporating crossing rate reverses the study's main conclusion at 32B scale, while threshold-dependent metrics excluding crossing rate consistently select deep layers where all four models show significant HS advantage (Holm-Bonferroni corrected; precision +15.4 to +32.5pp over MiniLM-384d). This advantage is encoder-conditional: it narrows substantially against BGE-large-1024d (1/4 models significant).",
  "new_string": "The central finding is methodological: a composite layer selection metric incorporating crossing rate reverses the study's main conclusion at 32B scale, while threshold-dependent metrics excluding crossing rate consistently select deep layers where all four models pass Holm-Bonferroni correction (precision +15.4 to +32.5pp over MiniLM-384d). At 7--8B the advantage is robust; at 32B, model-specific caveats apply (QwQ-32B is predominantly position-mediated). The advantage is encoder-conditional: it narrows substantially against BGE-large-1024d (1/4 models significant)."
}
```

```json
{
  "replace_all": "False",
  "file_path": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_5/projects/contrastive_trace_safety/docs/paper/sections/abstract.tex",
```
```json
  "old_string": "Our central finding is methodological: the choice of layer selection metric can reverse a study's conclusions. A composite metric that incorporates crossing rate systematically selects shallow layers at 32B scale and masks the HS advantage entirely. With principled layer selection, HS probes achieve precision gains of +15.4 to +32.5 percentage points over 384d sentence embeddings across all four models (Holm-Bonferroni corrected). This advantage is encoder-conditional: against a competitive 1024d encoder, only 1/4 models retains significance, and the directional advantage narrows substantially. At 32B ($n{=}19$--$20$, sample-limited), model-specific caveats further qualify the results.",
  "new_string": "Our central finding is methodological: the choice of layer selection metric can reverse a study's conclusions. A composite metric that incorporates crossing rate systematically selects shallow layers at 32B scale and masks the HS advantage entirely. With principled layer selection, HS probes achieve precision gains of +15.4 to +32.5 percentage points over 384d sentence embeddings across all four models (Holm-Bonferroni corrected). This advantage is encoder-conditional: against a competitive 1024d encoder, only 1/4 models retain significance under Holm-Bonferroni, and the directional advantage narrows substantially. At 32B ($n{=}19$--$20$, sample-limited), model-specific caveats further qualify the results."
}
```

```json
{
  "replace_all": "False",
  "file_path": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_5/projects/contrastive_trace_safety/docs/paper/sections/related_work.tex",
  "old_string": "\\paragraph{Safety in Reasoning Models.}\nReasoning models introduce a qualitatively new failure mode: the model's own chain of thought can transition from safety-aware deliberation to harmful generation without adversarial input modification~\\cite{wang2025lrmsafety}. \\citet{harmthoughts} document this self-jailbreak phenomenon with step-level annotations, while \\citet{selfjailbreak} show that longer reasoning chains increase circumvention probability. \\citet{hagendorff2026lrm_jailbreak} demonstrate that reasoning models can autonomously jailbreak other LLMs with 97\\% success, identifying reasoning capability itself as a safety threat. H-CoT~\\cite{hcot2025} shows reasoning chains can be externally hijacked, complementing internally-originating transitions. Unfaithful reasoning~\\cite{chen2025unfaithful} further motivates hidden-state monitoring that bypasses text. On mitigation, SafeThink~\\cite{safethink} steers early reasoning toward safety, SafeRemind~\\cite{saferemind} inserts entropy-triggered reminders, and \\citet{korbak2025cot_monitorability} argue that CoT monitorability is valuable but fragile. \\citet{mao2025selfjailbreak_reasoning} further characterize self-jailbreak in reasoning models and propose Chain-of-Guardrails as a defense. \\citet{baker2025monitoring} show that CoT monitoring can detect reward hacking in reasoning models but warn that optimization pressure promotes obfuscation, complementing our focus on passive detection. \\citet{yin2025refusal_cliff} discover that safety-aligned reasoning models maintain refusal intentions during thinking but experience a sharp drop at output generation, a phenomenon our temporal probes could detect. \\citet{ahrend2026safer_traces} address information leakage in traces; \\citet{hu2026refusal_dynamics} trace refusal dynamics in standard LLMs. \\citet{xu2025manipulated} show that reasoning models can be manipulated without external attacks through self-generated reasoning artifacts. GuardReasoner~\\cite{xiang2025guardreasoner} proposes a lightweight real-time guard model for reasoning LLMs, complementing our probe-based approach with a dedicated detection architecture. \\citet{su2025reasoning_tokens} unify reasoning tokens and safety in a single framework. None conducts a systematic modality comparison for commitment detection.",
  "new_string": "\\paragraph{Safety in Reasoning Models.}\nReasoning models introduce a qualitatively new failure mode: the model's own chain of thought can transition from safety-aware deliberation to harmful generation without adversarial input modification~\\cite{wang2025lrmsafety}.\n\n\\textit{Attack characterization.} Self-jailbreak has been documented with step-level annotations~\\cite{harmthoughts}, with longer chains increasing circumvention probability~\\cite{selfjailbreak}. Reasoning models can autonomously jailbreak other LLMs with 97\\% success~\\cite{hagendorff2026lrm_jailbreak}, and can be manipulated through self-generated reasoning artifacts~\\cite{xu2025manipulated}. H-CoT~\\cite{hcot2025} shows reasoning chains can be externally hijacked. Unfaithful reasoning~\\cite{chen2025unfaithful} further motivates hidden-state monitoring that bypasses text. Safety-aligned models maintain refusal intentions during thinking but experience a sharp drop at output generation~\\cite{yin2025refusal_cliff}, a phenomenon our temporal probes target. Refusal dynamics in standard LLMs~\\cite{hu2026refusal_dynamics} and information leakage in traces~\\cite{ahrend2026safer_traces} provide additional context.\n\n\\textit{Detection.} CoT monitoring can detect reward hacking but optimization pressure promotes obfuscation~\\cite{baker2025monitoring}. CoT monitorability is valuable but fragile~\\cite{korbak2025cot_monitorability}. GuardReasoner~\\cite{xiang2025guardreasoner} proposes a dedicated lightweight guard model; \\citet{su2025reasoning_tokens} unify reasoning tokens and safety in a single framework.\n\n\\textit{Mitigation.} SafeThink~\\cite{safethink} steers early reasoning toward safety, SafeRemind~\\cite{saferemind} inserts entropy-triggered reminders, and Chain-of-Guardrails~\\cite{mao2025selfjailbreak_reasoning} proposes guardrail insertion during reasoning. None of these conducts a systematic modality comparison for commitment detection."
}
```

H-CoT~\\cite{hcot2025} shows reasoning chains can be externally hijacked, complementing internally-originating transitions. Unfaithful reasoning~\\cite{chen2025unfaithful} further motivates hidden-state monitoring that bypasses text. On mitigation, SafeThink~\\cite{safethink} steers early reasoning toward safety, SafeRemind~\\cite{saferemind} inserts entropy-triggered reminders, and \\citet{korbak2025cot_monitorability} argue that CoT monitorability is valuable but fragile. \\citet{mao2025selfjailbreak_reasoning} further characterize self-jailbreak in reasoning models and propose Chain-of-Guardrails as a defense. \\citet{baker2025monitoring} show that CoT monitoring can detect reward hacking in reasoning models but warn that optimization pressure promotes obfuscation, complementing our focus on passive detection. \\citet{yin2025refusal_cliff} discover that safety-aligned reasoning models maintain refusal intentions during thinking but experience a sharp drop at output generation, a phenomenon our temporal probes could detect. \\citet{ahrend2026safer_traces} address information leakage in traces; \\citet{hu2026refusal_dynamics} trace refusal dynamics in standard LLMs. \\citet{xu2025manipulated} show that reasoning models can be manipulated without external attacks through self-generated reasoning artifacts. GuardReasoner~\\cite{xiang2025guardreasoner} proposes a lightweight real-time guard model for reasoning LLMs, complementing our probe-based approach with a dedicated detection architecture. \\citet{su2025reasoning_tokens} unify reasoning tokens and safety in a single framework. None conducts a systematic modality comparison for commitment detection.",
```json
    "new_string": "\\paragraph{Safety in Reasoning Models.}\nReasoning models introduce a qualitatively new failure mode: the model's own chain of thought can transition from safety-aware deliberation to harmful generation without adversarial input modification~\\cite{wang2025lrmsafety}.\n\n\\textit{Attack characterization.} Self-jailbreak has been documented with step-level annotations~\\cite{harmthoughts}, with longer chains increasing circumvention probability~\\cite{selfjailbreak}. Reasoning models can autonomously jailbreak other LLMs with 97\\% success~\\cite{hagendorff2026lrm_jailbreak}, and can be manipulated through self-generated reasoning artifacts~\\cite{xu2025manipulated}. H-CoT~\\cite{hcot2025} shows reasoning chains can be externally hijacked. Unfaithful reasoning~\\cite{chen2025unfaithful} further motivates hidden-state monitoring that bypasses text. Safety-aligned models maintain refusal intentions during thinking but experience a sharp drop at output generation~\\cite{yin2025refusal_cliff}, a phenomenon our temporal probes target. Refusal dynamics in standard LLMs~\\cite{hu2026refusal_dynamics} and information leakage in traces~\\cite{ahrend2026safer_traces} provide additional context.\n\n\\textit{Detection.} CoT monitoring can detect reward hacking but optimization pressure promotes obfuscation~\\cite{baker2025monitoring}. CoT monitorability is valuable but fragile~\\cite{korbak2025cot_monitorability}. GuardReasoner~\\cite{xiang2025guardreasoner} proposes a dedicated lightweight guard model; \\citet{su2025reasoning_tokens} unify reasoning tokens and safety in a single framework.\n\n\\textit{Mitigation.} SafeThink~\\cite{safethink} steers early reasoning toward safety, SafeRemind~\\cite{saferemind} inserts entropy-triggered reminders, and Chain-of-Guardrails~\\cite{mao2025selfjailbreak_reasoning} proposes guardrail insertion during reasoning. None of these conducts a systematic modality comparison for commitment detection."
  }
}
```

_source line 5305 · 2026-05-18T11:32:34.771803+00:00_

88	\end{table}
89	
90	R1-32B detects commitment 11--13 steps early with a +14.2pp margin over the position baseline, which confirms genuine representational signal, but covers only 25\% of traces at the single significant window ($W{=}1$). QwQ-32B achieves statistical significance at all window sizes with 74\% coverage, but HS probes fire later than text probes (negative lead time). The position baseline achieves higher balanced accuracy than HS probes (0.832 vs.\ 0.796) because harmful steps tend to occur later in traces. HS probes achieve directionally higher precision (+4.5pp: 0.564 vs.\ 0.519, $p{=}0.133$) and lower FPR (+2.5pp: 0.217 vs.\ 0.241, $p{=}0.279$), but neither difference is statistically significant at $n{=}19$. A position-residualized probe (regressing out $t/T$ from hidden states before classification) reduces QwQ-32B balanced accuracy by 18.3pp (from 0.796 to 0.612), but remains significantly above chance (per-trace balanced accuracy 0.667, $p{<}0.003$, Wilcoxon signed-rank; \cref{app:position_residual}). Hidden states encode some position-independent safety signal, but the majority of the HS probe's discriminative power on this model derives from temporal position encoding. The divergence implies that per-model validation is essential: no single detection configuration generalizes even within the same architecture at the same scale, and a system validated on one 32B model would fail on the other.
91	
92	\subsection{Temporal Context}\label{sec:temporal}

61	\end{table}
62	
63	Against the 384d baseline, HS probes significantly outperform text probes on all four models across all three metrics (\cref{tab:hs_vs_text}). At 7--8B, the evidence is strong: precision gains of +32.5pp (R1-8B) and +21.9pp (OT-7B), stable across seeds (std ${<}4$pp). R1-8B FPR advantage is directionally consistent across seeds (5/5) but not robust to data split (1/5 $p{<}0.05$; \cref{app:multi_seed}). At 32B, both models are significant (HB corrected) but with model-specific caveats: R1-32B achieves significance only at $W{=}1$ with 25\% coverage, and QwQ-32B's position baseline is competitive (\cref{sec:32b-analysis}). Control task selectivity confirms genuine safety signal: 12.5--19.7pp above chance (\cref{app:control}).
64	
65	\paragraph{Dimensionality control.} PCA reduction of HS features to 384d (matching text encoder dimensionality, 79--92\% variance retained) preserves the precision advantage on all four models (+16.8 to +23.1pp, all $p{<}0.012$), with all four model-level precision tests passing Holm-Bonferroni. Two individual metric comparisons are non-significant: R1-8B balanced accuracy (+1.3pp, $p{=}0.731$, effect too small at $n{=}43$) and QwQ-32B FPR ($-$11.4pp, $p{=}0.038$, below HB threshold). This confirms a modality effect rather than a capacity artifact.

_source line 5309 · 2026-05-18T11:32:35.478170+00:00_

1	\section{Limitations}\label{sec:limitations}
2	
3	\paragraph{32B model-specific caveats.}
4	R1-32B achieves significance only at $W{=}1$ (25\% coverage, 5/20 traces); dimensionality ($51200 \times W$ for $n{=}20$) prevents larger windows. QwQ-32B's position baseline achieves higher balanced accuracy (0.832 vs.\ HS 0.796), HS lead time is negative, and position-residualized probing reduces balanced accuracy by 18.3pp. The QwQ-32B HS advantage is largely position-mediated. Multi-seed analysis (\cref{app:multi_seed}) confirms scale-dependent robustness: 7--8B precision std ${<}8$pp across 5 seeds, while R1-32B std $= 12.8$pp. This motivates a two-tier framing: 7--8B results are robust; 32B results are pilot-scale with caveats.
5	
6	\paragraph{High false positive rate and oracle gap.}
7	Safe-trace FPR is 39--52\% at standard thresholds, so current probes characterize the phenomenon rather than constitute production detectors. The 85.5\% oracle gap confirms that passive probing captures only a fraction of available signal.
8	
9	\paragraph{Cumulative hyperparameter selection.}
10	Layer (up to 64), $W$ (7 values), $K$ (5 values), and threshold are selected on the same validation set ($n{=}4$--$9$ per-class validation traces at 32B scale), yielding up to $64 \times 7 \times 5 = 2{,}240$ configurations. $C$-insensitivity (${<}2$pp) covers one hyperparameter; the cumulative researcher degrees of freedom are substantial, particularly at 32B where per-fold sample sizes are small. $K$ was set as a design pre-selection (\cref{app:k_ablation}), and the $K \times W$ joint surface is not fully explored. Joint (layer, $W$) stability was verified via grid search for QwQ-32B ($W \in \{1,3,5\}$; \cref{app:layer_sweep}), but full leave-one-fold-out validation of the (layer, $W$) pair and extension to R1-32B remain for future work. Bootstrap CIs at $n{=}19$--$20$ may exhibit below-nominal coverage~\citep{efron1993bootstrap}; $p$-value resolution with 10{,}000 resamples is $10^{-4}$.

358	\end{equation}
359	where $\text{Acc}_{\text{linguistic}}$ is the probe accuracy on true labels and $\text{Acc}_{\text{control}}$ is the accuracy on permuted labels. A selectivity of zero indicates that the probe exploits only non-linguistic features (e.g., position); higher values confirm that the representation encodes task-relevant information.
360	
361	\paragraph{Results.} We repeat the control experiment 5 times (10 for OT-7B) with different random permutations and report the mean control accuracy. Selectivity scores at corrected layers are: R1-32B 19.7pp (layer 63), OT-7B 18.3pp (layer 16), QwQ-32B 14.4pp (layer 60), R1-8B 12.5pp (layer 14). All values exceed the 5pp threshold recommended by \cite{hewitt2019control}. R1-32B achieves the strongest selectivity (19.7pp) at the corrected layer, a 2.6$\times$ increase from 7.5pp at the biased layer L10. OT-7B achieves the second-highest selectivity at a mid-depth transformer layer (57\%), consistent with R1-8B's pattern at 44\% depth. On QwQ-32B, the position baseline accuracy (0.705) slightly exceeds the HS probe (0.667) at L60, consistent with the main-text finding that QwQ's HS advantage is concentrated in precision and FPR rather than balanced accuracy.
362	
363	\paragraph{Layer provenance note.} R1-8B (L14) and OT-7B (L16) are unaffected by the layer selection bias because their biased and corrected layers coincide. QwQ-32B selectivity is 14.4pp at L60 (comparable to 15.3pp at L2), confirming that safety-specific signal persists at deep layers.

12	
13	    \item \textbf{Encoder-conditional directional advantage.} Against MiniLM-384d, all four models show significant HS precision gains (+15.4 to +32.5pp, Holm-Bonferroni corrected). The advantage persists after PCA dimensionality matching (all four precision tests pass at 384d) but narrows substantially against BGE-large-1024d (1/4 models retains significance), so the advantage is encoder-conditional rather than absolute (\cref{fig:forest_precision}). At 7--8B scale (moderate samples, robust across 5 seeds), evidence is strong; at 32B ($n{=}19$--$20$ test traces, sample-limited), model-specific caveats apply: QwQ-32B's HS advantage is largely position-mediated (residualized probe drops 18.3pp), and the remaining precision (+4.5pp, $p{=}0.133$) and FPR (+2.5pp, $p{=}0.279$) margins over the position baseline are not statistically significant at $n{=}19$ (\cref{sec:32b-analysis}).

_source line 5329 · 2026-05-18T11:33:28.857336+00:00_

593	\label{app:position_residual}
594	
595	To quantify position-independent safety signal in QwQ-32B hidden states, we regress out the normalized position $t/T$ from each hidden-state vector before training the logistic regression probe. The residualized probe achieves pooled step-level balanced accuracy of 0.619 and per-trace balanced accuracy of 0.667 (16/19 traces with BA $> 0.5$). Three complementary significance tests confirm the residualized probe performs above chance: Wilcoxon signed-rank $p{=}0.0024$, paired $t$-test $p{=}0.0008$, permutation test $p{<}0.001$. The bootstrap 95\% CI for per-trace balanced accuracy is $[0.575, 0.744]$.
596	
597	These results confirm that hidden states encode some position-independent safety signal. However, the drop from full-probe balanced accuracy (0.796) to residualized (0.619, pooled) indicates that the majority of discriminative power derives from temporal position encoding. The HS vs.\ position-baseline precision gap (+4.5pp, $p{=}0.133$) and FPR gap (+2.5pp, $p{=}0.279$) are not statistically significant at $n{=}19$, so the non-positional component, while real, is too small for reliable detection at pilot scale.
598	
599	
600	\section{Multi-Seed Stability}

592	\section{QwQ-32B Position-Residualized Probe}
593	\label{app:position_residual}
594	
595	To quantify position-independent safety signal in QwQ-32B hidden states, we regress out the normalized position $t/T$ from each hidden-state vector before training the logistic regression probe. The residualized probe achieves pooled step-level balanced accuracy of 0.619 and per-trace balanced accuracy of 0.667 (16/19 traces with BA $> 0.5$). Three complementary significance tests confirm the residualized probe performs above chance: Wilcoxon signed-rank $p{=}0.0024$, paired $t$-test $p{=}0.0008$, permutation test $p{<}0.001$. The bootstrap 95\% CI for per-trace balanced accuracy is $[0.575, 0.744]$.
596	
597	These results confirm that hidden states encode some position-independent safety signal. However, the drop from full-probe balanced accuracy (0.796) to residualized (0.619, pooled) indicates that the majority of discriminative power derives from temporal position encoding. The HS vs.\ position-baseline precision gap (+4.5pp, $p{=}0.133$) and FPR gap (+2.5pp, $p{=}0.279$) are not statistically significant at $n{=}19$, so the non-positional component, while real, is too small for reliable detection at pilot scale.
598	
599	
600	\section{Multi-Seed Stability}
601	\label{app:multi_seed}
602	
603	\cref{tab:multi_seed} reports LR probe performance across 5 random seeds at each model's corrected layer and optimal $W$.
604

## W1 [Critical]: Encoder-conditional 从 caveat 升为核心发现

### Abstract
- 384d 和 1024d 结果并列呈现（不是"结论+例外"）
- 保留 "+15.4 to +32.5pp over 384d" 作为 magnitude anchor
- 1024d caveat 扩展为对等表述，不少于 384d 篇幅

### Introduction Contribution 2
- 384d 和 1024d 结果给予平等权重（不是 3 个 bullet 讨论 384d + 1 句提 1024d）

### Discussion 新增段落（以下结构严格遵循）：
1. 承认问题："Part of the observed advantage may reflect text encoder quality rather than hidden-state informational superiority."
2. PCA-384d 反驳纯容量解释："At matched 384d dimensionality (79-92% variance retained), precision advantage persists on all four models (+16.8 to +23.1pp, 10/12 HB pass), eliminating capacity gap as the sole explanation."
3. 承认 BGE-1024d："A stronger 1024d encoder closes most of the significance gap (1/4 HB), suggesting a continuum."
4. 总结："The HS advantage has two separable components: a robust modality effect (survives dimensionality matching) and an encoder-dependent magnitude component (narrows with stronger baselines)."

### Method/Experiments
- "dimensionality control" → "capacity control" 全文替换
- 加一句说明 PCA 最大方差方向 ≠ text encoder 语义空间

## W2 [Critical]: 两级 framing
- **Abstract 不改**（保持 "sample-limited"，不加 "preliminary"）
- Discussion/Limitations: 明确分层 "7-8B: robust across 5 seeds; 32B: sample-limited with model-specific caveats"

## W3 [Major]: CP 标注不确定性
- Limitations 加 1 段：CP 标注方法论来自 HarmThoughts 原始论文，但本文未评估 IAA。如 CP 标注误差 ≥2 steps，报告的 lead time (+4.5 to +13 steps) 可能部分在标注噪声内。引用 HarmThoughts 原始方法论。

## W4 [Major]: QwQ claim
- **保留 "all four (HB corrected)"**，不改 "three of four"
- 但确保 caveat 每次出现在 "all four" 旁边：Abstract 加 parenthetical "though QwQ-32B's advantage is predominantly position-mediated (§4.5)"
- 确认当前 §4.2 和 Conclusion 的措辞已有此 caveat

## W5 [Major]: R1-32B 绝对数字
- 检查 experiments.tex 是否已有 "25% coverage (5/20 traces)" 明文。如没有，在 §4.3 或 Table 4 附近加。
- 加 1 句讨论：low coverage 下显著性的实际含义。

## W6 [Major]: Deployment language 条件化
- discussion.tex: "We recommend temporal windowing (W=3-15)... as deployment defaults" → "If future work achieves acceptable false positive rates, our results suggest temporal windowing (W=3-15)... as a starting configuration"
- Table 4 caption: "early warning" → "limited early detection" 或类似审慎措辞
- Section 4.5 "waste intervention resources" → 保持，但加 "in a hypothetical deployment scenario" context

## W7 [Minor]: R1-8B FPR 跨 seed 脆弱性
- 主文 "all 12 tests pass" 后加注："though R1-8B FPR significance is fragile across seeds (2/5 uncorrected, 1/5 Bonferroni-corrected; Table L.2)"

## W8 [Minor]: GRU validation 策略
- 附录 GRU 段：明确 70/30 split 下 validation 策略。是否从 70% 中再分 val？如果 validation = test，标注并讨论信息泄漏可能。

## W10 [Minor]: 补 probing 引用
- Related Work "Probing Methodology" 段补充：
  - Jawahar et al. (2019) — What Does BERT Look At?
  - Liu et al. (2019, NAACL) — Linguistic Knowledge and Transferability
  - Voita & Titov (2020) — Information-Theoretic Probing with MDL
  - Pimentel et al. (2020, ACL) — Information-Theoretic Probing for Linguistic Structure
- 先 WebSearch 获取正确 bib 条目

## W11 [Minor]: RepE vs LR 参数化
- Section 4.6 RepE 段加 1-2 句：RepE uses class-conditioned mean difference (analytic solution), while LR optimizes cross-entropy (iterative). The key distinction is parameterization, not supervision.

_source line 5355 · 2026-05-18T12:04:35.949483+00:00_

1	\section{Introduction}\label{sec:introduction}
2	
3	Reasoning models such as DeepSeek-R1~\cite{deepseekr1} and QwQ~\cite{qwq} scale inference-time computation through extended chains of thought (CoT). Recent work documents that these models can ``self-jailbreak'': without adversarial input modification, the CoT transitions from safety-aware deliberation to committed harmful generation~\cite{harmthoughts,selfjailbreak}. We define the first reasoning step after which safety deliberation permanently ceases as the \textbf{Safety Commitment Point} (CP). This failure mode differs qualitatively from adversarial jailbreaks on standard LLMs because the harmful transition originates within the model's own reasoning. Before building production detectors, a prior question must be answered: can current probing methods reliably distinguish pre-CP from post-CP reasoning steps, and under what conditions? This paper provides a systematic characterization of what hidden-state and text-based probes can and cannot detect.
4	
5	Existing safety detectors target fundamentally different problems. Hidden-state (HS) methods such as TrajGuard~\cite{trajguard} and SafeSwitch~\cite{safeswitch} classify adversarial inputs at the token level, while text monitors like SafeRemind~\cite{saferemind} scan generated reasoning for explicit violations. Neither tracks the gradual, multi-step commitment process. A fundamental question remains: should a monitoring system read the model's hidden states or its generated text? This comparison has never been conducted under controlled conditions. Na\"ive reasoning suggests HS features (up to 5120 dimensions) should dominate text embeddings (384d sentence encoder), but no prior work tests this systematically. A further complication is that HS probes require selecting which transformer layer to monitor, a choice rarely scrutinized but potentially decisive.
6	
7	We present the first systematic evaluation across four reasoning models spanning two architectures (L
…[truncated 2963 chars]

2	
3	We evaluate temporal probes across four reasoning models (\cref{tab:models}), comparing hidden-state (HS) and text modalities under different layer selection criteria.
4	
5	\subsection{Setup}\label{sec:setup}
6	
7	We study R1-8B (Llama-8B, R1-distilled, $n{=}43$ test traces), OT-7B (OpenThinker-7B, trained on the OpenThoughts dataset~\citep{openthinker}; Qwen-7B backbone, STILL-trained, $n{=}47$), QwQ-32B (Qwen-32B, native reasoning, $n{=}19$--$20$), and R1-32B (Qwen-32B, R1-distilled, $n{=}20$). All traces come from HarmThoughts with step-level CP annotations (60/20/20 split). HS probes extract last-token hidden states from a selected layer; text probes encode each step with all-MiniLM-L6-v2 (384d). Both use L2-regularized logistic regression with temporal windowing $W \in \{1, 3, 5, 10, 15, 20, 25\}$ selected on validation. Detection requires $K{=}5$ consecutive threshold crossings. Statistical testing follows \cref{sec:evaluation}.

9	\subsection{Layer Selection Results}\label{sec:layer-results}
10	
11	\cref{tab:layer_selection} shows that the biased composite metric (balanced accuracy $\times$ crossing rate) systematically selects shallow layers for 32B models: L2 (3\% depth) for QwQ-32B and L10 (16\%) for R1-32B. Three of four metrics excluding crossing rate (precision, inverse FPR, and additive composite) consistently select deep layers: L60 (94\%) and L63 (98\%). Pure balanced accuracy, a threshold-free metric, selects middle layers (L30 and L47), consistent with the AUROC pattern discussed in \cref{sec:discussion}. The mechanism is that shallow layers produce highly compressed representations with small inter-step margins: step-to-step cosine distances are 1.4--2.7$\times$ smaller than at deep layers across all four models (\cref{app:cosine_volatility}). 1	\appendix
2	
3	\section{Responsible NLP Research Checklist}
4	\label{app:checklist}
5	
6	We address the ARR Responsible NLP Research requirements below.
7	
8	\begin{itemize}
9	    \item \textbf{Limitations discussed:} Yes (\cref{sec:limitations}).
10	    \item \textbf{Potential risks:} This research analyzes jailbreak traces to understand how reasoning models commit to generating harmful content. We do not develop new attack methods or release jailbreak prompts. All analyzed content comes from the publicly available HarmThoughts benchmark. We acknowledge a dual-use concern: safety probes that detect commitment could in principle be used to craft reasoning traces that evade detection. We believe the defensive value of understanding commitment dynamics outweighs this risk, as evasion requires white-box access to the probe, which a deployed monitoring system would not expose.
11	    \item \textbf{Computational budget:} Our experiments involve 4 models with 209, 228, 100, and 95 traces respectively (632 total), using linear probes and single-layer GRUs. Total compute is minimal (under 4 GPU-hours on a single RTX A6000). No large-scale pretraining or finetuning was performed.
12	    \item \textbf{Data:} We use HarmThoughts, a publicly available benchmark with step-level safety annotations. No new data collection was performed.
13	    \item \textbf{Human subjects:} No human subjects research was conducted.
14	    \item \textbf{Reproducibility:} All hyperparameters are reported in \cref{app:gru_details,app:bocpd_config}. Code and corrected data will be released upon publication.
15	\end{itemize}
16

_source line 5361 · 2026-05-18T12:04:46.794800+00:00_

1	Reasoning models with chain-of-thought can self-jailbreak: their reasoning traces transition from safe deliberation to committed harmful generation without adversarial input. We present the first systematic comparison of hidden-state (HS) probes and text-based monitors for detecting this safety commitment, evaluated across four reasoning models spanning two architectures and two scales (7--8B, 32B).
2	
3	Our central finding is methodological: the choice of layer selection metric can reverse a study's conclusions. A composite metric that incorporates crossing rate (the frequency of threshold crossings) systematically selects shallow layers at 32B scale and masks the HS advantage entirely. With principled layer selection, HS probes achieve precision gains of +15.4 to +32.5 percentage points over 384d sentence embeddings across all four models (Holm-Bonferroni corrected). This advantage is encoder-conditional: against a competitive 1024d encoder, only 1/4 models retain significance under Holm-Bonferroni, and the directional advantage narrows substantially. At 32B ($n{=}19$--$20$, sample-limited), model-specific caveats further qualify the results.
4	
5	Our results characterize the safety commitment phenomenon rather than constitute production detectors: false positive rates remain 39--52\%, and no alternative method consistently matches supervised probing. Layer selection methodology is a critical, underexamined researcher degree of freedom in the probing literature.
6

1	\section{Discussion}\label{sec:discussion}
2	
3	\paragraph{Layer selection as researcher degree of freedom.}
4	Layer selection is an underexamined methodological choice in the probing literature that can silently determine a study's conclusions. Our five-metric ablation (\cref{sec:layer-results}) demonstrates that a single metric component, the crossing rate, reverses the central finding: at biased layers, the HS advantage appears scale-dependent; at corrected layers, all four models are significant (\cref{sec:evaluation}). The mechanism is representation compression at shallow layers (\cref{app:cosine_volatility}), which pushes a linear probe to the noise floor and inflates crossing rate independently of discriminative power. Any composite metric that incorporates crossing rate is susceptible to this bias, and we hypothesize the effect generalizes beyond safety probing to any threshold-based detection study using crossing rate for layer selection. Validation on other probing tasks is needed to confirm generality.
5	
6	\paragraph{Recommendations for probing methodology.} We recommend that probing studies report results under at least two complementary layer selection criteria, and that five-metric ablations (or at minimum, results at multiple candidate layers spanning shallow, middle, and deep positions) become standard practice. Even among unbiased metrics, the choice between threshold-based and threshold-free criteria affects layer selection: AUROC tends to select layers at shallower depth (3--50\%) than threshold-dependent metrics (44--98\%). Pure balanced accuracy exhibits the same pattern, selecting layers at 47--73\% depth for 32B models rather than the 94--98\% selected by threshold-dependent metrics. This divergence between threshold-free and threshold-dependent criteria further demonstrates that layer selection methodology is a critical researcher degree of freedom. At AUROC-optimal layers, HS probes retain directional precision advantage on all four mode

1	\section{Limitations}\label{sec:limitations}
2	
3	\paragraph{32B model-specific caveats.}
4	R1-32B achieves significance only at $W{=}1$ (25\% coverage, 5/20 traces); dimensionality ($51200 \times W$ for $n{=}20$) prevents larger windows. QwQ-32B's position baseline achieves higher balanced accuracy (0.832 vs.\ HS 0.796), HS lead time is negative, and position-residualized probing reduces balanced accuracy by 17.7pp. The QwQ-32B HS advantage is largely position-mediated. Multi-seed analysis (\cref{app:multi_seed}) confirms scale-dependent robustness: 7--8B precision std ${<}8$pp across 5 seeds, while R1-32B std $= 12.8$pp. This motivates a two-tier framing: 7--8B results are robust; 32B results are sample-limited with caveats.
5	
6	\paragraph{High false positive rate and oracle gap.}
7	Safe-trace FPR is 39--52\% at standard thresholds, so current probes characterize the phenomenon rather than constitute production detectors. The 85.5\% oracle gap confirms that passive probing captures only a fraction of available signal.
8	
9	\paragraph{Cumulative hyperparameter selection.}
10	Layer (up to 64), $W$ (7 values), $K$ (5 values), and threshold are selected on the same validation set ($n{=}4$--$9$ per-class validation traces at 32B scale), yielding up to $64 \times 7 \times 5 = 2{,}240$ configurations. $C$-insensitivity (${<}2$pp) covers one hyperparameter; the cumulative researcher degrees of freedom are substantial, particularly at 32B where per-fold sample sizes are small. $K$ was set as a design pre-selection (\cref{app:k_ablation}), and the $K \times W$ joint surface is not fully explored. Joint (layer, $W$) stability was verified via grid search for QwQ-32B ($W \in \{1,3,5\}$; \cref{app:layer_sweep}), but full leave-one-fold-out validation of the (layer, $W$) pair and extension to R1-32B remain for future work. Bootstrap CIs at $n{=}19$--$20$ may exhibit below-nominal coverage~\citep{efron1993bootstrap}; $p$-value resolution with 10{,}000 resamples is $10^{-4}$.

1	\section{Related Work}\label{sec:related-work}
2	
3	\paragraph{Safety in Reasoning Models.}
4	Reasoning models introduce a qualitatively new failure mode: the model's own chain of thought can transition from safety-aware deliberation to harmful generation without adversarial input modification~\cite{wang2025lrmsafety}.
5	
6	\textit{Attack characterization.} Self-jailbreak has been documented with step-level annotations~\cite{harmthoughts}, with longer chains increasing circumvention probability~\cite{selfjailbreak}. Reasoning models can autonomously jailbreak other LLMs with 97\% success~\cite{hagendorff2026lrm_jailbreak}, and can be manipulated through self-generated reasoning artifacts~\cite{xu2025manipulated}. H-CoT~\cite{hcot2025} shows reasoning chains can be externally hijacked. Unfaithful reasoning~\cite{chen2025unfaithful} further motivates hidden-state monitoring that bypasses text. Safety-aligned models maintain refusal intentions during thinking but experience a sharp drop at output generation~\cite{yin2025refusal_cliff}, a phenomenon our temporal probes target. Refusal dynamics in standard LLMs~\cite{hu2026refusal_dynamics} and information leakage in traces~\cite{ahrend2026safer_traces} provide additional context.
7	
8	\textit{Detection.} CoT monitoring can detect reward hacking but optimization pressure promotes obfuscation~\cite{baker2025monitoring}. CoT monitorability is valuable but fragile~\cite{korbak2025cot_monitorability}. GuardReasoner~\cite{xiang2025guardreasoner} proposes a dedicated lightweight guard model; \citet{su2025reasoning_tokens} unify reasoning tokens and safety in a single framework.
9	
10	\textit{Mitigation.} SafeThink~\cite{safethink} steers early reasoning toward safety, SafeRemind~\cite{saferemind} inserts entropy-triggered reminders, and Chain-of-Guardrails~\cite{mao2025selfjailbreak_reasoning} proposes guardrail insertion during reasoning. None of these conducts a systematic modality comparison for commitment detection.
11
