Multi-seed analysis (\\cref{app:multi_seed}) confirms scale-dependent robustness: 7--8B precision std ${<}8$pp across 5 seeds, while R1-32B std $= 12.8$pp. This motivates a two-tier framing: 7--8B results are robust; 32B results are sample-limited with caveats.",
```json
  "new_string": "\\paragraph{Model-specific caveats.}\nR1-32B achieves significance only at $W{=}1$ (25\\% coverage, 5/20 traces); dimensionality ($51200 \\times W$ for $n{=}20$) prevents larger windows, and joint (layer, $W$) grid search was not conducted for R1-32B ($n{=}20$ is the fundamental bottleneck). R1-32B's HS advantage is position-mediated: the residualized HS-text gap is non-significant (+1.9pp, $p{=}0.914$; \\cref{app:position_residual}). QwQ-32B's position baseline achieves higher balanced accuracy (0.832 vs.\\ HS 0.796) and HS lead time is negative. R1-8B FPR significance is seed-fragile (3/5 HB). Robustness varies by model (OT-7B 5/5 HB, $\\Delta$Prec std${=}1.3$pp; R1-8B 3/5 HB, std${=}4.5$pp), not by scale."
```
```json
}
```

_source line 5705 · 2026-05-18T13:58:08.163610+00:00_

49	\subsection{Evaluation Protocol}
50	\label{sec:evaluation}
51	
52	We report balanced accuracy, precision, pre-CP FPR (step-level and trace-level), coverage, and lead time (steps between first detection and true CP). Detection requires $K{=}5$ consecutive threshold crossings; $K$ was set as a design pre-selection before examining performance, and sensitivity analysis (\cref{app:k_ablation}) confirms that $K \in \{3, 5, 7\}$ produces smooth coverage-FPR tradeoffs without qualitative changes in the modality comparison. Modality comparisons use two-sided null-centered bootstrap (10{,}000 resamples) with Holm-Bonferroni step-down correction (3 metrics $\times$ 4 models $= 12$ tests, $\alpha{=}0.05$). Within each model, the three metrics are positively correlated by construction, making the correction stricter than under independence. Control task selectivity~\citep{hewitt2019control} validates genuine representational structure (\cref{app:control}).
53

598	\centering
599	\small
600	\begin{tabular}{lcrrrr}
601	\toprule
602	\textbf{Model} & $W$ & \textbf{HS BA} & \textbf{Resid.\ HS BA} & \textbf{Drop} & \textbf{Resid.\ Text BA} \\
603	\midrule
604	R1-8B & 15 & 0.834 & 0.669 & 16.5pp & 0.605 \\
605	R1-8B & 3 & 0.813 & 0.571 & 24.3pp & 0.534 \\
606	OT-7B & 3 & 0.732 & 0.609 & 12.3pp & 0.540 \\
607	QwQ-32B & 3 & 0.796 & 0.619 & 17.7pp & --- \\
608	\bottomrule
609	\end{tabular}
610	\caption{Position-residualized probe balanced accuracy. All residualized HS probes significantly above chance ($p{<}0.001$). HS residualized probes retain more non-positional signal than text residualized probes (+3.7 to +6.9pp).}
611	\label{tab:position_residual}
612	\end{table}
613	
614	Position is a significant component across all models (12--18pp BA reduction at optimal $W$), but all models retain substantial non-positional signal (residualized BA 0.61--0.67, all $p{<}0.001$). Larger temporal windows reduce position dependence: R1-8B drops 24.3pp at $W{=}3$ but only 16.5pp at $W{=}15$, indicating that broader context captures more non-positional structure.
615	
616	For QwQ-32B specifically, three complementary significance tests confirm the residualized probe performs above chance: Wilcoxon signed-rank $p{=}0.0024$, paired $t$-test $p{=}0.0008$, permutation test $p{<}0.001$ (bootstrap 95\% CI for per-trace BA: $[0.575, 0.744]$). The position baseline achieves higher balanced accuracy (0.832 vs.\ HS 0.796), and HS precision (+4.5pp, $p{=}0.133$) and FPR (+2.5pp, $p{=}0.279$) margins over the position baseline are not statistically significant at $n{=}19$.
617	
618	
619	\section{Multi-Seed Stability}
620	\label{app:multi_seed}
621	
622	\cref{tab:multi_seed} reports LR probe performance across 5 random seeds at each model's corrected layer and optimal $W$.

641	
642	R1-32B's high FPR variance (std $= 0.145$) reflects a single outlier seed (FPR $= 0.48$), amplified by the small test set ($n{=}20$). At 7--8B scale, all metrics are stable (precision std ${<}8$pp, FPR std ${<}3$pp), supporting robust conclusions.
643	
644	\paragraph{R1-8B FPR per-seed breakdown.} \cref{tab:r18b_fpr_seeds} reports per-seed HS vs.\ text FPR comparisons for R1-8B. The HS advantage is directionally consistent (5/5 seeds), with 2/5 reaching $p{<}0.05$ uncorrected; after Bonferroni correction for 5 seeds ($\alpha{=}0.01$), 1/5 remains significant. Text FPR is highly stable (std $= 0.007$) while HS FPR is split-sensitive (std $= 0.040$), which explains why the canonical-split FPR advantage ($-7.4$pp) does not generalize robustly across seeds.

```json
  "old_string": "\\paragraph{Per-model evidence and design implications.}\nRobustness splits by model, not scale. OT-7B provides the cleanest evidence (5/5 seeds HB, largest residualized gap +6.9pp, highest selectivity 18.3pp). QwQ-32B is robust (5/5 HB, residualized gap +3.7pp significant) but its position baseline is competitive. R1-8B precision is stable ($\\Delta$Prec std${=}4.5$pp, residualized gap +6.4pp) but FPR is seed-fragile (3/5 HB). R1-32B is statistically stable (5/5 HB, $\\Delta$Prec std${=}2.0$pp) but its HS advantage is position-mediated: the residualized HS-text gap is +1.9pp ($p{=}0.914$), non-significant (\\cref{app:position_residual}). Distillation and native reasoning training thus produce qualitatively different safety representation geometry within the same architecture. If future work achieves acceptable false positive rates, temporal windowing ($W{=}3$--$15$) with principled layer selection provides a reasonable starting range. PCA capacity control, control task selectivity, and position residualization (3/4 models retain significant non-positional HS advantage) provide converging evidence that the HS advantage reflects genuine safety representations in most models; direct erasure methods (LEACE; \\citealp{belrose2023leace}) remain untested.",
  "new_string": "\\paragraph{Per-model evidence and design implications.}\nRobustness splits by model, not scale: OT-7B and QwQ-32B (5/5 seeds HB each) are most stable, R1-8B FPR is seed-fragile (3/5 HB), and R1-32B passes all seeds but its advantage is position-mediated (residualized gap NS; \\cref{app:position_residual}). Distillation and native reasoning training produce qualitatively different safety representation geometry within the same architecture. If future work achieves acceptable false positive rates, temporal windowing ($W{=}3$--$15$) with principled layer selection provides a reasonable starting range. PCA capacity control, control task selectivity, and position residualization (3/4 models retain significant non-positional HS advantage) provide converging evidence of genuine safety representations; direct erasure methods (LEACE; \\citealp{belrose2023leace}) remain untested."
}
```

[Director 指令] [补充 Patch — D039 范围修正]

Reviewer 刚确认 R1-32B 残差化数据，有一个需要修正的数字：

**D039 position drop 范围**：论文中如有引用 "12-18pp position mediation across scales" 的表述，需改为 **"9-20pp"** 或分开说明：
- R1-8B W=15: 16.5pp
- OT-7B W=3: 12.3pp  
- QwQ-32B W=3: 9.1pp (canonical 口径 17.7pp)
- R1-32B W=1: **20.4pp** (W=1 无 temporal context，position 影响更大)

建议 Discussion 中如讨论 position mediation，用 "position accounts for 9-20pp of balanced accuracy depending on model and window size" 或类似表述，并注脚说明 R1-32B 的 W=1 是特殊情况。

**residualized gap 表述**：Discussion 中 "HS retains +3.7 to +6.9pp non-positional signal" 的范围仅适用于三个模型。R1-32B 需单独说明："+1.9pp, p=0.914, n=20, consistent with position-mediated advantage"。

这是对已派 R12 综合重写的增量修正，其余指令不变。

_source line 5769 · 2026-05-18T14:02:59.333102+00:00_

3	\paragraph{Model-specific caveats.}
4	R1-32B achieves significance only at $W{=}1$ (25\% coverage, 5/20 traces); dimensionality ($51200 \times W$ for $n{=}20$) prevents larger windows, and joint (layer, $W$) grid search was not conducted for R1-32B ($n{=}20$ is the fundamental bottleneck). R1-32B's HS advantage is position-mediated: the residualized HS-text gap is non-significant (+1.9pp, $p{=}0.914$; \cref{app:position_residual}). QwQ-32B's position baseline achieves higher balanced accuracy (0.832 vs.\ HS 0.796) and HS lead time is negative. R1-8B FPR significance is seed-fragile (3/5 HB). Robustness varies by model (OT-7B 5/5 HB, $\Delta$Prec std${=}1.3$pp; R1-8B 3/5 HB, std${=}4.5$pp), not by scale.

_source line 5773 · 2026-05-18T14:03:09.173022+00:00_

12	\paragraph{Per-model evidence and design implications.}
13	Robustness splits by model, not scale: OT-7B and QwQ-32B (5/5 seeds HB each) are most stable, R1-8B FPR is seed-fragile (3/5 HB), and R1-32B passes all seeds but its advantage is position-mediated (residualized gap NS; \cref{app:position_residual}). Distillation and native reasoning training produce qualitatively different safety representation geometry within the same architecture. If future work achieves acceptable false positive rates, temporal windowing ($W{=}3$--$15$) with principled layer selection provides a reasonable starting range. PCA capacity control, control task selectivity, and position residualization (3/4 models retain significant non-positional HS advantage) provide converging evidence of genuine safety representations; direct erasure methods (LEACE; \citealp{belrose2023leace}) remain untested.
